You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.
</collaboration_mode>

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

# AGENTS.md

This file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.

## Fast Locator

- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`
- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`
- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`

If an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

## Project Map

- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.
- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.
- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.
- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.
- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.
- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.
- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.
- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.

## Stack And Commands

- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.
- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.
- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.
- Build: `npm run build`
- Lint: `npm run lint`
- Test: `npm run test`
- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`
- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.
- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).

## Working Rules

- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.
- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.
- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.
- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.
- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.
- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.
- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.
- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.

## Product Constraints

- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.
- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.
- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.
- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.
- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.

## Smart Drill Note

On `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.

## Verification (must not skip)

- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.
- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.

## Workflow Expectations

- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.
- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.
- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.
- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.
- **After every successful push, publish through Lovable's manual Publish control.** Do not prompt the Lovable agent, use a credit-spending action, or change anything else in Lovable. Verify that the manual publish completed before reporting the change as live.
- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.

## Session Protocol (second brain)

Product context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.

The vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student's record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.

- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.
- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.

`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.

## Codex Adapter Layout

- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.
- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.
- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.

This checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.

## Known Gaps

- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.
- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.
- `README.md` is still minimal.

## Agent skills

### Issue tracker

GitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.

### Triage labels

The five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.

### Domain docs

Multi-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.

Home = `/classroom` in the Classroom frame (`src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md`).\\r\\n- Home code: `src/components/classroom/home/` - `useClassroomHome.ts`, `homeModel.ts` (pure, tested), `ClassroomHomeView.tsx`, `home.css`.\\r\\n- ~25 one-to-one students plus Joshua. A teacher is never the student.\\r\\n- Done separately: PR #132 (sideways scrollbar).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Goal\\r\\nA sleek, high-end Home that (1) gets a student into practice in one click, (2) shows the performance insight that matters most for THIS student\'s recent work, and (3) puts every message and notification in one place.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Approach\\r\\n\\r\\n### 0. Feature-preservation checklist (applies to every phase)\\r\\nThe redesign must keep, until a named replacement ships:\\r\\n- Assigned work rows and their actions (`ClassroomHomeView.tsx:168`).\\r\\n- Timed-section link interception that shows the Section briefing (`ClassroomHomeView.tsx:197`, `SectionBriefing.tsx`).\\r\\n- Report-card seen tracking (`ClassroomHomeView.tsx:362`, `reportCardSeen.ts`).\\r\\n- Home message preview dismissal watermark.\\r\\n- Access gates in `HomeAccess` (messages, schedule, activities, drill): a hidden feature stays hidden.\\r\\n- Next seven days strip: markup, classes and behavior unchanged.\\r\\nExisting `ClassroomHomeView.test.tsx` cases stay green; new cases added per phase.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Layout\\r\\nTop to bottom; two columns decided by the Home container\'s available width (CSS container query), not the viewport, so a docked side panel collapses it to one column.\\r\\n1. **Practice launcher** (the existing `QuestionFinder`, extended; one instance, one Ctrl/Cmd+K owner, lazy bank loading and focus trap kept) + inbox bell + greeting.\\r\\n2. **Three one-click actions**:\\r\\n   - Continue assigned work: the earliest-due eligible assigned row (not overdue-dismissed, not teacher-addressed); hidden when none. Timed sections still route through the briefing.\\r\\n   - Smart Drill: `kind: \'smart-drill\'` (adaptive, unchanged).\\r\\n   - Start a timed section: opens the launcher on its Section tab.\\r\\n3. **Performance area**: one main insight + up to three minor ones (fewer when evidence is thin).\\r\\n4. **Inbox panel** (right column): unified feed, Joshua\'s human-written messages pinned.\\r\\n5. **Next session** card (dark).\\r\\n6. **Next seven days**: unchanged.\\r\\n- Look: light and warm, dark cards for emphasis. Motion: count-up, settle-in, gentle pulse; off under `prefers-reduced-motion`.\\r\\n- Empty states: one line + one action.\\r\\n- Verification matrix: student account at narrow (390), mid (900), wide (1440), and side-panel-docked widths; keyboard-only pass; reduced motion; dark class (`html.dark`).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Practice launcher (Joshua\'s \\"specific question / dropdown\\" idea)\\r\\nOne panel, three tabs, all through the existing launch codec (`src/lib/drillLaunch.ts`):\\r\\n- **Question**: PrepTest \\u001a Section \\u001a Question dropdowns plus free text (exists). Contextual action changes with depth: PT+Section selected \\u001a \\"Start section\\"; +Question \\u001a \\"Open question\\". LR/RC routing stays the finder\'s.\\r\\n- **Section**: bank-aware dispatch: an LR section launches `kind: \'section\'` (LR bank, `Drill.tsx:540`); an RC section does NOT reuse the finder\'s single-passage link (`passage=<id>` opens one passage with `DEFAULT_CONFIG`, `RcPlayer.tsx:130`). New RC section launch: resolve every passage of that PT section in order, create an RC session with those passage ids and the chosen timer config (the player already accepts multiple ids and explicit config, `RcPlayer.tsx:141`), then navigate by session id. PrepTest + Section, annotated with freshness (\\"24 of 25 unanswered\\", `sectionFreshness.ts:46`), fresh-only toggle, and the student\'s timer setting (standard / 52.5 / 70 / custom / untimed) carried into `kind: \'section\'` `timerMode`. RC freshness shown as unknown until RC history is connected.\\r\\n- **By type**: question-type multi-select + count + optional difficulty \\u001a `kind: \'filter\'` (`drillLaunch.ts:163`). Adaptive Smart Drill is never repurposed for this. \\"Fresh only\\" needs an exposure-aware path: add an optional `excludeAnswered?: boolean` to the filter body (additive, old links unaffected), resolved when the queue is built (`drillRunSpec.ts:146`) against the student\'s answered qids; the built queue is stored in the Run Record so resume replays the same questions. If fewer fresh questions exist than `count`, say \\"Only N new Flaw questions left\\" before start, with an option to include answered ones.\\r\\n- Shelves (search-matched in the same panel): Flagged (`flaggedService.ts`) and Needs review (journal review status). Decided: yes (Joshua, 2026-09-23).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Insight engine (fixed rules, no AI text)\\r\\nPure `pickInsights(signals, pin?)` in `src/components/classroom/home/insights.ts`, fixture-tested. Adapters feed it, one per source; the engine never reads Supabase.\\r\\n\\r\\nSource-to-lens matrix:\\r\\n| Lens | Source / reader | Coverage label |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| LR question types | NEW additive RPC `home_lr_type_accuracy(p_class_id, p_days int default 30)`: latest attempt per distinct question in window, excluding review/blind-review replays; returns qtype, questions, correct, last_attempt_at. Companion RPC `home_lr_type_evidence(p_class_id, p_qtype, p_days)` shares the SAME selection function, returning the qids behind the number | \\"LR practice, last 30 days\\" |\\r\\n| Sections | Home adapter over `section_history`, NOT the unchanged `sectionHistory.ts` reader (it zero-fills missing `initial_total`/`initial_percent`/`total_time_ms` and stamps missing dates as now, `sectionHistory.ts:113`). Adapter derives totals from `total_questions`/`initial_score`, leaves unknown dates unknown (excluded from recency), never infers timing | \\"Sections\\" (\\"Timed\\" only when `mode` proves it) |\\r\\n| Section diagnosis | saved `analysis_json` via `sectionAnalysis.ts` categories (reading / fundamentals / process / test-taking); Home summarizes the top existing finding and links to its report | \\"From your last section\\" |\\r\\n| RC | `src/features/rc/api/rcProgress.ts` | \\"Reading Comprehension\\" |\\r\\n| Bootcamp | per-topic progress stores (e.g. `topics/*/lib/supabase-progress.ts`) | \\"Bootcamp: <name>\\" |\\r\\n| Assigned work | `useAssignedTodoFeed` status only: done / due / overdue counts. No scores inferred from copy | \\"Work Joshua assigned\\" |\\r\\n| Repeat mistakes | `wrong_answer_journal` by qtype; trap wording from `wajNotesService` notes where present | \\"Wrong Answer Journal\\" |\\r\\n| Pace / confidence | `attempts.time_ms`, `attempts.confidence` (LR only) | \\"LR practice\\" |\\r\\n\\r\\nSelection rules:\\r\\n- Each lens returns `null` (ineligible) or `{ score 0..1, evidence }`. Eligibility floors per lens (e.g. qtype: �8 distinct questions; sections: �1 completed in 21 days; RC: �2 passages in 21 days).\\r\\n- Score = 0.5 x recency (days since last activity in that source, decays over 14 days) + 0.5 x actionability (size of the gap the lens found, normalized per lens). Volume never compared across units.\\r\\n- Ties broken by a fixed lens order. Redundancy: at most one card per source; a section cannot feed both Sections and Section diagnosis unless the diagnosis names something the score card does not.\\r\\n- 0 eligible \\u001a a single \\"Start here\\" card (Smart Drill). 1-3 eligible \\u001a show only those.\\r\\n- Pinned lens becomes main if eligible; if ineligible, show it as main with its empty state + the engine\'s picks as minors, and flag it in Joshua\'s Hub as \\"pinned view has no data\\".\\r\\n- Every insight has: coverage label, sample size, date range, an **evidence drawer**, and actions. Evidence is source-specific: question addresses for LR types (evidence RPC) and sections (`questions_json`); module list for bootcamp; the assignment rows for assigned work; passage list for RC.\\r\\n- From a section diagnosis, \\"Practice this exact skill\\": when the finding\'s type/feature ids match launch filters (`sectionAnalysis.ts:160` to `kind: \'filter\'` `qtypes`/`qfeatures`), prefill and show the filters before launch. For a weak LR type: \\"Try new ones\\" (`kind: \'filter\'`, fresh only) and, when journal entries exist, \\"Review these\\" (journal). Pairing decided: yes (Joshua, 2026-09-23).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Pinning\\r\\nAdditive table `home_insight_pins`: `class_id text primary key references profiles(class_id)`, `lens text not null check (lens in (<enum>))`, `params jsonb not null default \'{}\'`, `pinned_by uuid not null default auth.uid()`, `updated_at timestamptz default now()`.\\r\\nRLS: admin SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE (via existing admin role check); student SELECT where `class_id` = their own profile\'s class_id. Params validated per lens in the client and by a CHECK on known keys. Unpin = delete row. Hub control on the student\'s page. RLS tests: student cannot read another student\'s pin or write their own.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Unified inbox\\r\\n- New, separately named hook `useHomeFeed()` (existing `useInbox` untouched), on TanStack Query keys shared by Home panel and bell.\\r\\n- **Event identity**: additive nullable `event_key text` on BOTH `notifications` and individual `messages`, plus `origin text check in (\'human\',\'system\')` on individual messages (not threads). Generated writers (`classroomNotify.ts:87`, `sessionBundleNotify.ts:48`, new writers) compute one deterministic key per event (e.g. `assignment:<id>:assigned`) and insert both rows with it; partial unique indexes `(recipient, event_key) where event_key is not null` on each table make retries no-ops. Human-typed messages get `origin=\'human\'`, no key.\\r\\n- **Protection**: extend the notifications immutability trigger (`20260506124235_...sql:120`) to cover `event_key`/`origin`, and the same on messages; tests prove a student cannot change either.\\r\\n- **One canonical visible-event model**, computed server-side in one RPC `home_feed(p_limit, p_before)`: an event with a key appears once (message wins, notification suppressed); legacy rows without keys use the 5-minute same-link heuristic inside the same RPC. The badge count comes from the same RPC, so counts never include hidden rows.\\r\\n- **Read**: two separate read states, never mixed. (a) Conversation read = the existing participant `last_read_at` watermark (`useInbox.ts:247`), advanced ONLY when the student opens the conversation itself. (b) Generated events = a new additive `event_acks (user_id, event_key, acked_at)` table; opening an assignment/recap/grade/reminder event acks its key, which hides its notification duplicate and marks the generated message as read for the feed, without moving the conversation watermark. A generated message with no notification twin is acked the same way. Unread for a thread row = unread human messages after the watermark; generated messages count as unread until acked. Mark-all-read acks visible events and advances watermarks only for threads shown. RLS: student reads/writes own acks only.\\r\\n- **Thread rows**: preview is Joshua\'s latest unread human message when one exists, else the latest message; unread = any unread human message from Joshua in the thread, not only the latest (the latest-only check in `useInbox.ts:189` is not reused).\\r\\n- Hidden types (`streak`, `signup`) filtered in SQL before paging; page size 30 with load more.\\r\\n- New notification types need the DB CHECK constraint extended (additive). Writers:\\r\\n  | Event | Writer | Status |\\r\\n  |---|---|---|\\r\\n  | New assignment | `classroomNotify.ts` | exists, add key/origin |\\r\\n  | Notes / recaps | `sessionBundleNotify.ts` | exists, add key/origin |\\r\\n  | Graded work | grading completion (`activity_section_grades` flow) | new |\\r\\n  | Session reminder | none today (type defined, no producer, verified). New scheduled job: 24h and 1h before `start_at`; key `session:<booking_id>:<offset>`; skipped when cancelled or moved, re-keyed on reschedule | new; needs a scheduler (Edge Function cron via Lovable, confirm with Joshua) |\\r\\n  | Booking change | booking Edge Function | new, Lovable deploy |\\r\\n\\r\\n### 6. Build order (each its own PR, each passes the preservation checklist)\\r\\n1. Layout + restyle, existing data only; launcher tabs Question + Section; actions; inbox panel showing today\'s message preview; Next seven days untouched.\\r\\n2. Insight engine: LR types (new RPC), Sections, Section diagnosis; evidence drawer.\\r\\n3. Unified feed + bell + dedup columns + notification query fixes.\\r\\n4. RC, bootcamp, assigned, mistakes, pace/confidence lenses; By-type launcher tab; pinning.\\r\\n5. New notification writers: graded work; session reminders (scheduled job); booking changes. Reminders and booking need Lovable Edge Function deploys, confirmed with Joshua first.\\r\\n6. Teacher view adopts the design.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Key decisions & tradeoffs\\r\\n- Rules, not AI text: predictable, never states a false thing.\\r\\n- Source adapters + coverage labels instead of one \\"all practice\\" number: honest, more code.\\r\\n- Separate `useHomeFeed` instead of rewriting `useInbox`: zero risk to current consumers, some duplication.\\r\\n- Dedup via additive `event_key`/`origin` + a legacy heuristic, instead of back-filling old rows: no data changes.\\r\\n- Container-query layout instead of viewport breakpoints.\\r\\n- Next seven days frozen.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Risks / open questions\\r\\n- Performance: Home adds RPC + section history + feed. Plan: parallel queries, skeletons, no waterfall; measure first contentful paint before/after on a student account.\\r\\n- Legacy dedup heuristic may miss or over-hide; accept for legacy only.\\r\\n- Exact decay and floor values need tuning on real student data (fixtures from 3 anonymized students).\\r\\n- Product decisions (Joshua, 2026-09-23: yes to all three): Flagged and Needs review shelves in the launcher, Needs review ordered by the student\'s reattempt_due_at with next_action shown; Review these next to Try new ones on weak-area insights; session reminders 24h and 1h before, via a scheduled job (Lovable deploy, confirm the route before deploying).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Out of scope\\r\\n- Public landing page. Goal score, streaks, latest PT, recent drills list, month calendar. AI insight text. Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \\"review since X\\".\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Standards** - does the code conform to this repo\'s documented coding standards?\\r\\n- **Spec** - does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?\\r\\n\\r\\nBoth axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don\'t pollute each other\'s context, then this skill aggregates their findings.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe issue tracker should have been provided to you - run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Process\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Pin the fixed point\\r\\n\\r\\nWhatever the user said is the fixed point - a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn\'t specify one, ask for it.\\r\\n\\r\\nCapture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here - not inside two parallel sub-agents.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Identify the spec source\\r\\n\\r\\nLook for the originating spec, in this order:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) - fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n2. A path the user passed as an argument.\\r\\n3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.\\r\\n4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn\'t one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report \\"no spec available\\".\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Identify the standards sources\\r\\n\\r\\nAnything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel\\r\\n\\r\\nSend a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Standards sub-agent prompt** - include:\\r\\n\\r\\n- The full diff command and commit list.\\r\\n- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.\\r\\n- The brief: \\"Report - per file/hunk where relevant - every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n**Spec sub-agent prompt** - include:\\r\\n\\r\\n- The diff command and commit list.\\r\\n- The path or fetched contents of the spec.\\r\\n- The brief: \\"Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn\'t asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Aggregate\\r\\n\\r\\nPresent the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings - the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).\\r\\n\\r\\nEnd with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don\'t pick a single winner across axes - that\'s the reranking the separation exists to prevent.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why two axes\\r\\n\\r\\nA change can pass one axis and fail the other:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing \\u001a **Standards pass, Spec fail.**\\r\\n- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project\'s conventions \\u001a **Spec pass, Standards fail.**\\r\\n\\r\\nReporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.\\r\\n"}'}]

(os error 123)\\nimport { supabase } from \\"@/integrations/supabase/client\\";\\r\\nimport { sendAssignmentEmail } from \\"@/lib/assignmentEmail\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Notify a student that a new classroom_items row has been created for them.\\r\\n * Posts a message to the 1:1 admin\\u001dstudent inbox thread (creating it if\\r\\n * needed), inserts a bell notifications row, and emails the student.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Non-throwing. Returns granular flags so callers can surface partial-success\\r\\n * states truthfully (\\"Assigned, but email failed\\").\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Email is sent via the `send-assignment-email` edge function, which uses\\r\\n * Resend. If RESEND_API_KEY or FROM_EMAIL is unset the function returns\\r\\n * a truthful failure - we never fake email_ok.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface NotifyResult {\\r\\n  inbox_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  bell_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  email_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  inbox_error?: string;\\r\\n  bell_error?: string;\\r\\n  email_error?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface NotifyParams {\\r\\n  adminUserId: string;\\r\\n  studentId: string;\\r\\n  title: string;\\r\\n  /** Path the student should land on, e.g. /classroom/item/<id> */\\r\\n  route: string;\\r\\n  /** Short body shown in the inbox message + bell notification (optional). */\\r\\n  description?: string;\\r\\n  /** Type label used in the email body, e.g. \\"homework\\", \\"bootcamp_module\\". */\\r\\n  assignmentType?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function findOrCreateThread(adminUserId: string, studentId: string): Promise<string> {\\r\\n  const { data: myParts, error: myPartsErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n    .select(\\"conversation_id\\")\\r\\n    .eq(\\"user_id\\", adminUserId);\\r\\n  if (myPartsErr) throw myPartsErr;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const myConvIds = (myParts ?? []).map((p) => p.conversation_id);\\r\\n  if (myConvIds.length > 0) {\\r\\n    const { data: shared, error: sharedErr } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n      .select(\\"conversation_id\\")\\r\\n      .eq(\\"user_id\\", studentId)\\r\\n      .in(\\"conversation_id\\", myConvIds)\\r\\n      .limit(1);\\r\\n    if (sharedErr) throw sharedErr;\\r\\n    if (shared && shared.length > 0) return shared[0].conversation_id;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { data: conv, error: cErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversations\\")\\r\\n    .insert({ subject: null, created_by: adminUserId })\\r\\n    .select()\\r\\n    .single();\\r\\n  if (cErr || !conv) throw cErr ?? new Error(\\"Failed to create conversation\\");\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { error: pErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"conversation_participants\\")\\r\\n    .insert([\\r\\n      { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: adminUserId },\\r\\n      { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: studentId },\\r\\n    ]);\\r\\n  if (pErr) throw pErr;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return conv.id;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function notifyStudentOfClassroomItem(\\r\\n  params: NotifyParams,\\r\\n): Promise<NotifyResult> {\\r\\n  const { adminUserId, studentId, title, route, description, assignmentType } = params;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const result: NotifyResult = {\\r\\n    inbox_ok: false,\\r\\n    bell_ok: false,\\r\\n    email_ok: false,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const conversationId = await findOrCreateThread(adminUserId, studentId);\\r\\n    const body =\\r\\n      `New assignment: ${title}\\\\n\\\\n` +\\r\\n      (description ? `${description}\\\\n\\\\n` : \\"\\") +\\r\\n      `View in your Classroom: ${route}`;\\r\\n    const { error: mErr } = await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\\"messages\\")\\r\\n      .insert({\\r\\n        conversation_id: conversationId,\\r\\n        sender_id: adminUserId,\\r\\n        body,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    if (mErr) throw mErr;\\r\\n    result.inbox_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\\"[classroomNotify:inbox] failed\\", { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.inbox_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \\"inbox failed\\";\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const { error: nErr } = await (supabase as unknown as {\\r\\n      from: (t: string) => {\\r\\n        insert: (v: unknown) => Promise<{ error: { message: string } | null }>;\\r\\n      };\\r\\n    })\\r\\n      .from(\\"notifications\\")\\r\\n      .insert({\\r\\n        user_id: studentId,\\r\\n        type: \\"assignment\\",\\r\\n        title: `New assignment: ${title}`,\\r\\n        body: description ?? null,\\r\\n        link: route,\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    if (nErr) throw nErr;\\r\\n    result.bell_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\\"[classroomNotify:bell] failed\\", { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.bell_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \\"bell failed\\";\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Email step. Failure is recorded but the assignment is not affected.\\r\\n  const emailRes = await sendAssignmentEmail({\\r\\n    studentId,\\r\\n    title,\\r\\n    description,\\r\\n    route,\\r\\n    assignmentType: assignmentType ?? \\"assignment\\",\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  result.email_ok = emailRes.ok;\\r\\n  if (!emailRes.ok) result.email_error = emailRes.error;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return result;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One-line summary suitable for a toast.\\r\\n *   - \\"Assigned and notified\\"            (all three ok)\\r\\n *   - \\"Assigned, but email failed\\"        (inbox+bell ok, email failed)\\r\\n *   - \\"Assigned, partial notification\\"    (some other partial)\\r\\n *   - \\"Assigned, but notification failed\\" (all three failed)\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function summarizeNotifyResult(r: NotifyResult): string {\\r\\n  if (r.inbox_ok && r.bell_ok && r.email_ok) return \\"Assigned and notified\\";\\r\\n  if (!r.inbox_ok && !r.bell_ok && !r.email_ok) return \\"Assigned, but notification failed\\";\\r\\n  if (r.inbox_ok && r.bell_ok && !r.email_ok) return \\"Assigned, but email failed\\";\\r\\n  return \\"Assigned, partial notification\\";\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\\r\\nimport type { User } from \'@supabase/supabase-js\';\\r\\nimport { getOrCreateAdminStudentConversation } from \'@/lib/adminConversation\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Session-note notifier (build-spec \\u00156). Distinct from\\r\\n * `notifyStudentOfClassroomItem`, which hardcodes \\"New assignment:\\" copy and is\\r\\n * correct only for homework/assignment parts. A session recap is NOT an\\r\\n * assignment, so it gets its own copy and notification type.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Posts a chat message authored AS Joshua into the 1:1 thread and a bell\\r\\n * notification, both pointing at the recap in the student\'s Classroom. Reuses\\r\\n * the existing admin\\u001dstudent conversation resolver and the notifications table.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Non-throwing per channel: returns granular flags so partial success is\\r\\n * surfaced truthfully, never faked. No source labels (\\"AI\\", \\"transcript\\",\\r\\n * \\"Gemini\\") ever appear in the copy.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface SessionNotifyResult {\\r\\n  inbox_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  bell_ok: boolean;\\r\\n  inbox_error?: string;\\r\\n  bell_error?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface SessionNotifyParams {\\r\\n  admin: User;\\r\\n  studentId: string;\\r\\n  /** Path to the recap, e.g. /classroom/item/<id> */\\r\\n  route: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Optional approved chat copy authored in review. When omitted, a short\\r\\n   * default in the coaching voice is used. Never machine-labeled.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  message?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst DEFAULT_MESSAGE =\\r\\n  \'I left a recap from our session in your Classroom. Take a look when you get a chance, then we keep building from there.\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function notifyStudentOfSessionRecap(\\r\\n  params: SessionNotifyParams,\\r\\n): Promise<SessionNotifyResult> {\\r\\n  const { admin, studentId, route } = params;\\r\\n  const result: SessionNotifyResult = { inbox_ok: false, bell_ok: false };\\r\\n  const body = (params.message?.trim() || DEFAULT_MESSAGE) + `\\\\n\\\\n${route}`;\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const conversationId = await getOrCreateAdminStudentConversation(admin, studentId);\\r\\n    const { error: mErr } = await supabase.from(\'messages\').insert({\\r\\n      conversation_id: conversationId,\\r\\n      sender_id: admin.id,\\r\\n      body,\\r\\n    });\\r\\n    if (mErr) throw mErr;\\r\\n    await supabase\\r\\n      .from(\'conversations\')\\r\\n      .update({ last_message_at: new Date().toISOString() })\\r\\n      .eq(\'id\', conversationId);\\r\\n    result.inbox_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\'[sessionBundleNotify:inbox] failed\', { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.inbox_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \'inbox failed\';\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  try {\\r\\n    const { error: nErr } = await supabase.from(\'notifications\').insert({\\r\\n      user_id: studentId,\\r\\n      type: \'message\',\\r\\n      title: \'New recap in your Classroom\',\\r\\n      body: \'A recap from your latest session is ready.\',\\r\\n      link: route,\\r\\n    });\\r\\n    if (nErr) throw nErr;\\r\\n    result.bell_ok = true;\\r\\n  } catch (e) {\\r\\n    console.error(\'[sessionBundleNotify:bell] failed\', { studentId, error: e });\\r\\n    result.bell_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : \'bell failed\';\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return result;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}}'}]

question_types=ARRAY[$gd$Flaw$gd$]::text[], question_features=ARRAY[$gd$Famous Flaws$gd$]::text[], instruction_card=$gd${\\"left_md\\": \\"## The flaw is usually one of the famous ten\\\\n\\\\nMedium Flaw questions reward pattern recognition. The author makes a reasoning move that breaks in a predictable way, and the LSAT reuses the same handful of breaks again and again. We call these the **Famous Flaws**.\\\\n\\\\nThe ten that show up most:\\\\n- **Necessary vs. Sufficient** (a rule used backwards or negated)\\\\n- **Causation** (correlation treated as cause, reversed cause, third factor)\\\\n- **Sampling** (an unrepresentative group)\\\\n- **Equivocation** (one word, two meanings)\\\\n- **Percent vs. Amount** (a rate confused for a count)\\\\n- **Ad Hominem** (attacking the source, not the claim)\\\\n- **Circular** (the conclusion smuggled into the evidence)\\\\n- **Whole vs. Part**\\\\n- **Appeal to Authority / Popularity**\\\\n- **False Choice** (only two options when more exist)\\\\n\\\\nAt the medium level the trap answers also describe famous flaws. A wrong answer names a real flaw that this argument simply never made.\\", \\"right_md\\": \\"## Try these 5, then time them\\\\n\\\\nSet a timer for **8 minutes** (1.5x = 12, 2x = 16). Get an answer to all five, and flag any that felt like a low-confidence guess.\\\\n\\\\nFor each argument:\\\\n- Find the conclusion, then ask what the author had to assume to get there.\\\\n- Name the broken move in your own words before you read the answers.\\\\n- Match your prephrase to the famous-flaw list.\\\\n\\\\nWhen you read the answers:\\\\n- A choice can describe a real flaw and still be wrong because the argument never made it. Ask, did this argument actually do this?\\\\n- Some correct answers state the flaw in plain, specific language, not the textbook phrasing. Do not wait for the classic wording.\\\\n\\\\nAfter the timer, take a slow untimed second pass before you confirm. Note any flaw you keep missing.\\"}$gd$::jsonb, updated_at=now() WHERE slug=$gd$w8-01-famous-flaws-medium-set-1$gd$;\\r\\nsupabase/migrations\\\\20260620230000_guided_drills_lsatlab_backfill_and_import.sql:19:INSERT INTO public.guided_drills (slug,week,order_in_week,title,category,estimated_minutes,question_count,difficulty_min,difficulty_max,question_types,question_features,reasoning_types,start_button_label,instruction_card,bank_filter,source_file,is_published) VALUES ($gd$w10-famous-flaws-easier-set-2$gd$,10,8,$gd$Famous Flaws (Easier) Set 2$gd$,$gd$Pacing$gd$,30,5,1,2,ARRAY[$gd$Flaw$gd$]::text[],ARRAY[$gd$Famous Flaws$gd$]::text[],ARRAY[]::text[],$gd$Start$gd$,$gd${\\"left_md\\":\\"## Try 9**** **Famous Flaw** **** problems\\\\n**tests:** 60-64 / **levels:** 1-3\\\\n\\\\nGet better at spotting some famous flaw moves in the argument, and better at spotting some \\\\\\"decoy\\\\\\" famous flaws appearing in wrong answer choices.\\\\n\\\\nMany of the answers are not describing famous flaws. Some of the answer choices that are describing famous flaws will sound like the \\\\\\"classic\\\\\\" abstract language definition of that flaw, while others will use more unique or specific language to allude to the famous flaw.\\",\\"right_md\\":\\"- Set a timer for **14 mins** (1.5x = 21 mins, 2x = 28 mins)\\\\n\\\\n- Try to get an answer to all 12 of these problems.\\\\n\\\\n- Select your answer, but don\'t hit Confirm.\\\\n\\\\n- Flag any of them that feel like a struggle or a low-confidence guess.\\\\n\\\\n- When the timer goes off, flip back through them and revisit your thinking in an untimed way, before then hitting Confirm on your final answer.\\\\n\\\\n- Check answers and explanations.\\"}$gd$::jsonb,$gd${\\"qtypes\\":[\\"Flaw\\"],\\"features\\":[\\"Famous Flaws\\"],\\"reasoning_types\\":[],\\"difficulty_min\\":1,\\"difficulty_max\\":2,\\"count\\":5}$gd$::jsonb,$gd$lsatlab-studyplan$gd$,true)\\r\\nsupabase/migrations\\\\20260620230000_guided_drills_lsatlab_backfill_and_import.sql:25:INSERT INTO public.guided_drills (slug,week,order_in_week,title,category,estimated_minutes,question_count,difficulty_min,difficulty_max,question_types,question_features,reasoning_types,start_button_label,instruction_card,bank_filter,source_file,is_published) VALUES ($gd$w13-describing-arguments-match-family$gd$,13,1,$gd$Describing Arguments (Match Family)$gd$,$gd$Process$gd$,35,10,NULL,NULL,ARRAY[$gd$Role$gd$,$gd$Method$gd$,$gd$Main Conclusion$gd$]::text[],ARRAY[$gd$Describing Arguments$gd$]::text[],ARRAY[]::text[],$gd$Start$gd$,$gd${\\"left_md\\":\\"This 10 question set is comprised of three questions from the Match Family, which test our ability to understand what role certain claims are playing:\xa0\xa0Role, Method, and Main Conclusion.\\\\n\\\\n## **Role** and **Main Conclusion**\\\\n\\\\nMost questions \\\\\\"hide\\\\\\" the conclusion in one of two ways:\\\\n- it\'s the **very first claim** , or\\\\n- it\'s attached to some **pivot / rebuttal**\\\\n\\\\n**Opinion** indicators and **Pivot** indicators are very helpful for finding the parts of the argument.\\\\n\\\\nWhen we\'re assessing whether something is a conclusion (be it main conclusion or intermediate conclusion), we should ask ourselves,\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\"**Is this Supported** ? Why should we believe this?\\\\\\"\\\\nIf no support it provided, then it isn\'t a conclusion.\\\\n\\\\n## **Method**\\\\n\\\\nThese use a lot of abstract verbiage to describe parts of arguments. We want to not only learn how to read this type of language but also to start to think more in terms of this language. When you\'re using abstract language, you\'re speaking about general forms / archetypes, and the magic of getting better at standardized tests is getting better at recognizing the general forms / archetypes that show up a lot on that test.\\\\n\\\\nWe should get used to the most typical types of reasoning moves:\\\\n- **Analogies** / Distinctions\\\\n- Suggesting / Ruling out **Alternatives**\\\\n- **Faulty Implications** of a Claim or Plan\\",\\"right_md\\":\\"Length --\xa0\xa0**10 questions, 15 mins** (1.5x = 23, 2x = 30)****\\\\nTypes -- **Role** , **Method** , and **Main Conclusion**\\\\nTests -- **135, 140, and 123**\\\\nLevels -- **1 - 4**\\\\n\\\\n- Whenever you feel pretty low confidence or ambivalent about your guess, hit the FLAG / BOOKMARK icon to the left of the question stem. Unified inbox\\r\\n79:- New, separately named hook `useHomeFeed()` (existing `useInbox` untouched), on TanStack Query keys shared by Home panel and bell.\\r\\n80:- **Event identity**: additive nullable `event_key text` on BOTH `notifications` and individual `messages`, plus `origin text check in (\'human\',\'system\')` on individual messages (not threads). Generated writers (`classroomNotify.ts:87`, `sessionBundleNotify.ts:48`, new writers) compute one deterministic key per event (e.g. `assignment:<id>:assigned`) and insert both rows with it; partial unique indexes `(recipient, event_key) where event_key is not null` on each table make retries no-ops. Human-typed messages get `origin=\'human\'`, no key.\\r\\n81:- **Protection**: extend the notifications immutability trigger (`20260506124235_...sql:120`) to cover `event_key`/`origin`, and the same on messages; tests prove a student cannot change either.\\r\\n82:- **One canonical visible-event model**, computed server-side in one RPC `home_feed(p_limit, p_before)`: an event with a key appears once (message wins, notification suppressed); legacy rows without keys use the 5-minute same-link heuristic inside the same RPC. The badge count comes from the same RPC, so counts never include hidden rows.\\r\\n83:- **Read**: two separate read states, never mixed. (a) Conversation read = the existing participant `last_read_at` watermark (`useInbox.ts:247`), advanced ONLY when the student opens the conversation itself. A generated message with no notification twin is acked the same way. Unread for a thread row = unread human messages after the watermark; generated messages count as unread until acked. Mark-all-read acks visible events and advances watermarks only for threads shown. Build order (each its own PR, each passes the preservation checklist)\\r\\n96:1. Teacher view adopts the design.\\r\\n102:\\r\\n103:## Key decisions & tradeoffs\\r\\n104:- Rules, not AI text: predictable, never states a false thing.\\r\\n105:- Source adapters + coverage labels instead of one \\"all practice\\" number: honest, more code.\\r\\n106:- Separate `useHomeFeed` instead of rewriting `useInbox`: zero risk to current consumers, some duplication.\\r\\n107:- Dedup via additive `event_key`/`origin` + a legacy heuristic, instead of back-filling old rows: no data changes.\\r\\n108:- Container-query layout instead of viewport breakpoints.\\r\\n109:- Next seven days frozen.\\r\\n110:\\r\\n111:## Risks / open questions\\r\\n112:- Performance: Home adds RPC + section history + feed. Plan: parallel queries, skeletons, no waterfall; measure first contentful paint before/after on a student account.\\r\\n113:- Legacy dedup heuristic may miss or over-hide; accept for legacy only.\\r\\n114:- Exact decay and floor values need tuning on real student data (fixtures from 3 anonymized students).\\r\\n115:- Product decisions (Joshua, 2026-09-23: yes to all three): Flagged and Needs review shelves in the launcher, Needs review ordered by the student\'s reattempt_due_at with next_action shown; Review these next to Try new ones on weak-area insights; session reminders 24h and 1h before, via a scheduled job (Lovable deploy, confirm the route before deploying).\\r\\n116:\\r\\n117:## Out of scope\\r\\n118:- Public landing page. Changes to how Smart Drill or assignments run.\\r\\n"}}\n{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"adcd87","wall_time_seconds":0.7448154,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":774,"output":"8b4c406 feat(classroom-home): v2 layout, phase 1\\n6b7bf71 Deployed student archive fix\\n830a6f4 Changes\\n77d9af6 Changes\\n): HomeWeekDay[] {\\r\\n  const today = localDayISO(now);\\r\\n  const byDay = new Map<string, string[]>();\\r\\n  for (const entry of entries) {\\r\\n    const bucket = byDay.get(entry.iso);\\r\\n    if (bucket) bucket.push(entry.label);\\r\\n    else byDay.set(entry.iso, [entry.label]);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return dates.map((iso) => {\\r\\n    const date = parseLocalDay(iso);\\r\\n    const labels = byDay.get(iso) ?? [];\\r\\n    const weekday = date ? date.getDay() : 1;\\r\\n    return {\\r\\n      iso,\\r\\n      abbr: ABBR[weekday],\\r\\n      dayNum: date ? date.getDate() : 0,\\r\\n      isToday: iso === today,\\r\\n      isWeekend: weekday === 0 || weekday === 6,\\r\\n      // One line per cell: the first thing, and a count when there is more.\\r\\n      label:\\r\\n        labels.length === 0\\r\\n          ? null\\r\\n          : labels.length === 1\\r\\n            ? labels[0]\\r\\n            : `${labels[0]} +${labels.length - 1}`,\\r\\n    };\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Reading matter and completed Report Cards are not work to continue. */\\r\\nexport function pickContinueWork(rows: HomeRow[], access: HomeAccess, isAdmin: boolean): HomeRow | null {\\r\\n  if (isAdmin) return null;\\r\\n  const deadline = (row: HomeRow) => {\\r\\n    if (!row.dueAt) return Infinity;\\r\\n    const time = (parseLocalDay(row.dueAt) ?? new Date(row.dueAt)).getTime();\\r\\n    return Number.isFinite(time) ? time : Infinity;\\r\\n  };\\r\\n  return rows.filter((row) =>\\r\\n    ![\\"recap\\", \\"note\\"].includes(row.kind) &&\\r\\n    !row.key.startsWith(\\"report:\\") &&\\r\\n    (row.kind !== \\"activity\\" || access.activities) &&\\r\\n    (row.tone !== \\"drill\\" || access.drill)\\r\\n  ).sort((a, b) => deadline(a) - deadline(b))[0] ?? null;\\r\\n}\\r\\ndiff --git a/src/components/questionfinder/QuestionFinder.tsx b/src/components/questionfinder/QuestionFinder.tsx\\nindex 0465957..d0a869d 100644\\n--- a/src/components/questionfinder/QuestionFinder.tsx\\n+++ b/src/components/questionfinder/QuestionFinder.tsx\\n@@ -58,8 +58,13 @@ export interface QuestionFinderProps {\\n  * It never builds a set. Latest message per conversation (H22 proper fix).\\r\\n12:-- DISTINCT ON returns exactly one row per conversation_id: the most recent message.\\r\\n13:-- Shape matches the client\'s prior `{ id, conversation_id, body, created_at, sender_id }`\\r\\n14:-- so the inbox enrichment code is unchanged.\\r\\n15:create or replace function public.get_latest_messages(conv_ids uuid[])\\r\\n16:returns table (\\r\\n17:  conversation_id uuid,\\r\\n18:  id uuid,\\r\\n19:  body text,\\r\\n20:  created_at timestamptz,\\r\\n21:  sender_id uuid\\r\\n22:)\\r\\n23:language sql\\r\\n24:stable\\r\\n25:security invoker\\r\\n26:set search_path = public\\r\\n27:as $$\\r\\n28:  select distinct on (m.conversation_id)\\r\\n29:         m.conversation_id, m.id, m.body, m.created_at, m.sender_id\\r\\n30:  from messages m\\r\\n31:  where m.conversation_id = any(conv_ids)\\r\\n32:  order by m.conversation_id, m.created_at desc\\r\\n33:$$;\\r\\n34:\\r\\n35:-- 2. Conversations that have at least one attachment on ANY message.\\r\\n36:-- Replaces the stopgap paperclip check that only inspected the bounded set of\\r\\n37:-- recently-fetched messages (so an attachment older than the cutoff lost its\\r\\n38:-- indicator). This makes the indicator exact again while staying a single query.\\r\\n39:create or replace function public.get_conversations_with_attachments(conv_ids uuid[])\\r\\n40:returns table (conversation_id uuid)\\r\\n41:language sql\\r\\n42:stable\\r\\n43:security invoker\\r\\n44:set search_path = public\\r\\n45:as $$\\r\\n46:  select distinct m.conversation_id\\r\\n47:  from messages m\\r\\n48:  join message_attachments a on a.message_id = m.id\\r\\n49:  where m.conversation_id = any(conv_ids)\\r\\n50:$$;\\r\\n51:\\r\\n52:-- 3. Practice: light total/accuracy aggregate for a class.\\r\\n53:-- Practice only needs totalAttempted + avgAccuracy, so return the two counts and\\r\\n54:-- let the client divide. class_id is text (matches attempts.class_id).\\r\\n55:create or replace function public.attempt_totals(p_class_id text)\\r\\n56:returns table (total bigint, correct bigint)\\r\\n57:language sql\\r\\n58:stable\\r\\n59:security invoker\\r\\n60:set search_path = public\\r\\n61:as $$\\r\\n62:  select count(*)::bigint,\\r\\n63:         count(*) filter (where correct)::bigint\\r\\n64:  from attempts\\r\\n65:  where class_id = p_class_id\\r\\n66:$$;\\r\\n67:\\r\\n68:-- 4. Analytics: qtype x level x time-window rollup for a class.\\r\\n69:-- One grouped result reconstructs everything Analytics derived client-side:\\r\\n70:--   typeStats      = sum over levels + buckets, grouped by qtype\\r\\n71:--   levelStats     = sum over qtypes + buckets, grouped by level\\r\\n72:--   typeLevelStats = sum over buckets, grouped by (qtype, level)\\r\\n73:--   type7d         = rows where bucket = \'current7d\'\\r\\n74:--   typePrev7d     = rows where bucket = \'prev7d\'\\r\\n75:--   recentMissShare= (total - correct) per (qtype, level) across all buckets\\r\\n76:-- The bucket CASE mirrors the JS window test exactly: current7d = last 7 days,\\r\\n77:-- prev7d = the 7 days before that, older = everything else (incl. null timestamps,\\r\\n78:-- which fall through to \'older\' just as `new Date(null)` did client-side).\\r\\n79:create or replace function public.attempt_accuracy_rollup(p_class_id text)\\r\\n80:returns table (\\r\\n81:  qtype text,\\r\\n82:  level integer,\\r\\n83:  bucket text,\\r\\n84:  total bigint,\\r\\n85:  correct bigint\\r\\n86:)\\r\\n87:language sql\\r\\n88:stable\\r\\n89:security invoker\\r\\n90:set search_path = public\\r\\n91:as $$\\r\\n92:  select qtype,\\r\\n93:         level,\\r\\n94:         case\\r\\n95:           when timestamp_iso >= now() - Everywhere\\r\\n   * else the trigger stays where it was born: at the end of this row.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  withSearch?: boolean;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The Question jump: PrepTest, section, question — three dropdowns, always open.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The same three selectors used to be one panel deep, behind the finder\'s\\r\\n * trigger, which made the most ordinary request a student has (\\"open PT 90,\\r\\n * section 1, question 12\\") cost a click, a search field, and a decision about\\r\\n * what to type. LSAT Lab and 7Sage both put these three in the open, and so\\r\\n * does this: pick a test, pick a section, pick a question, and you are in it.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * It is a sibling of the finder, not a replacement. Addresses are what this\\r\\n * row does; searching question *text* is still the panel\'s job, and its trigger\\r\\n * sits at the end of the row.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The third select always lists official question numbers. LR opens the chosen\\r\\n * question in the drill runner; RC opens its passage at the chosen question.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport default function QuestionJump({\\r\\n  returnTo,\\r\\n  tone = \\"light\\",\\r\\n  bank,\\r\\n  withSearch = true,\\r\\n}: QuestionJumpProps) {\\r\\n  const navigate = useNavigate();\\r\\n  const { tests, loading, failed } = useFinderAddressIndex();\\r\\n\\r\\n  const [pt, setPt] = React.useState(\\"\\");\\r\\n  const [section, setSection] = React.useState(\\"\\");\\r\\n\\r\\n  const availableTests = React.useMemo(\\r\\n    () =>\\r\\n      bank\\r\\n        ? tests.filter((test) =>\\r\\n            test.sections.some((entry) =>\\r\\n              bank === \\"rc\\" ? entry.rc.length > 0 : entry.lr.length > 0,\\r\\n            ),\\r\\n          )\\r\\n        : tests,\\r\\n    [bank, tests],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  const sections = React.useMemo<FinderAddressSection[]>(() => {\\r\\n    if (!pt) return [];\\r\\n    const all = availableTests.find((test) => test.pt === Number(pt))?.sections ?? [];\\r\\n    if (!bank) return all;\\r\\n    return all.filter((entry) => (bank === \\"rc\\" ? entry.rc.length > 0 : entry.lr.length > 0));\\r\\n  }, [availableTests, bank, pt]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const activeSection = React.useMemo(\\r\\n    () => (section ? sections.find((s) => s.section === Number(section)) ?? null : null),\\r\\n    [section, sections],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  const isRc = !!activeSection && activeSection.lr.length === 0 && activeSection.rc.length > 0;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const rcQuestions = React.useMemo(\\r\\n    () =>\\r\\n      (activeSection?.rc ?? []).flatMap((passage) =>\\r\\n        (passage.questions ?? []).map((question) => ({ ...question, passage })),\\r\\n      ),\\r\\n    [activeSection],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  const thirdLabel = \\"Question number\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n  const openLr = (qnum: string) => {\\r\\n    const params = new URLSearchParams({\\r\\n      mode: \\"type-drill\\",\\r\\n      qids: `PT${pt}-S${section}-Q${qnum}`,\\r\\n      present: \\"smart\\",\\r\\n      returnTo,\\r\\n    });\\r\\n    navigate(`/drill?${params.toString()}`);\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  const openRc = (target: string) => {\\r\\n    const [passageId, questionId] = target.split(\\":\\");\\r\\n    const params = new URLSearchParams({ passage: passageId });\\r\\n    if (questionId) params.set(\\"q\\", questionId);\\r\\n    navigate(`/rc/drill?${params.toString()}`);\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  const thirdDisabled = loading || !activeSection;\\r\\n  const thirdPlaceholder = \\"Question number\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div className=\\"qj\\" data-tone={tone}>\\r\\n      <div className=\\"qj-row\\" data-search={withSearch ? \\"true\\" : \\"false\\"} role=\\"group\\" aria-label=\\"Jump to a question by PrepTest, section, and number\\">\\r\\n        <label className=\\"qf-field qj-field\\">\\r\\n          <span className=\\"qf-field-label\\">PrepTest</span>\\r\\n          <select\\r\\n            className=\\"qf-select\\"\\r\\n            value={pt}\\r\\n            disabled={loading || failed}\\r\\n            onChange={(event) => {\\r\\n              setPt(event.target.value);\\r\\n              setSection(\\"\\");\\r\\n            }}\\r\\n          >\\r\\n            <option value=\\"\\">\\r\\n              {failed ? \\"Unavailable\\" : loading ? \\"Loading…\\" : \\"PrepTest\\"}\\r\\n            </option>\\r\\n            {availableTests.map((test) => (\\r\\n              <option key={test.pt} value={test.pt}>\\r\\n                PT {test.pt}\\r\\n              </option>\\r\\n            ))}\\r\\n          </select>\\r\\n        </label>\\r\\n\\r\\n        <label className=\\"qf-field qj-field\\">\\r\\n          <span className=\\"qf-field-label\\">Section</span>\\r\\n          <select\\r\\n            className=\\"qf-select\\"\\r\\n            value={section}\\r\\n            disabled={loading || !pt}\\r\\n            onChange={(event) => setSection(event.target.value)}\\r\\n          >\\r\\n            <option value=\\"\\">Section</option>\\r\\n            {sections.map((entry) => (\\r\\n              <option key={entry.section} value={entry.section}>\\r\\n                Section {entry.section}\\r\\n                {entry.lr.length === 0 && entry.rc.length > 0 ? \\" · RC\\" : \\" · LR\\"}\\r\\n              </option>\\r\\n            ))}\\r\\n          </select>\\r\\n        </label>\\r\\n\\r\\n        {/* Value stays \\"\\" on purpose: this select is an action, not a stored\\r\\n            choice. Picking navigates away, and if the student comes back the\\r\\n            row should read as untouched rather than claim they are still\\r\\n            sitting on question 12. */}\\r\\n        <label className=\\"qf-field qj-field\\">\\r\\n          <span className=\\"qf-field-label\\">{thirdLabel}</span>\\r\\n          <select\\r\\n            className=\\"qf-select\\"\\r\\n            value=\\"\\"\\r\\n            disabled={thirdDisabled}\\r\\n            onChange={(event) => {\\r\\n              if (!event.target.value) return;\\r\\n              if (isRc) openRc(event.target.value);\\r\\n              else openLr(event.target.value);\\r\\n            }}\\r\\n          >\\r\\n            <option value=\\"\\">{thirdPlaceholder}</option>\\r\\n            {isRc\\r\\n              ? rcQuestions.map((question) => (\\r\\n                  <option key={question.id} value={`${question.passage.id}:${question.id}`}>\\r\\n                    Question {question.number} · Passage {question.passage.position}\\r\\n                  </option>\\r\\n                ))\\r\\n              : (activeSection?.lr ?? []).map((qnum) => (\\r\\n                  <option key={qnum} value={qnum}>\\r\\n                    Question {qnum}\\r\\n                  </option>\\r\\n                ))}\\r\\n          </select>\\r\\n        </label>\\r\\n\\r\\n        {withSearch ? (\\r\\n          <div className=\\"qj-search\\">\\r\\n            <QuestionFinder returnTo={returnTo} tone={tone} bank={bank} />\\r\\n          </div>\\r\\n        ) : null}\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"20170a","wall_time_seconds":0.8841393,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3383,"output":"/**\\r\\n * Supabase reads and writes for RC progress: sessions, attempts, annotations.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Content is static JSON (see rcBank.ts). Only the student\'s own work lives in\\r\\n * the database, and RLS scopes every row to them.\\r\\n */\\r\\nimport {\\r\\n  rcDb,\\r\\n  RC_ANNOTATIONS_TABLE,\\r\\n  RC_ATTEMPTS_TABLE,\\r\\n  RC_SESSIONS_TABLE,\\r\\n  type RcAttemptRow,\\r\\n  type RcDrillSessionRow,\\r\\n} from \'./rcSupabase.types\';\\r\\nimport type {\\r\\n  RcAttempt,\\r\\n  RcDrillConfig,\\r\\n  RcDrillSession,\\r\\n  RcHighlight,\\r\\n  RcLetter,\\r\\n  RcQuestionHistory,\\r\\n  RcSessionStatus,\\r\\n} from \'../lib/rcTypes\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Postgres \\"relation does not exist\\" - the migration has not been run. */\\r\\nconst MISSING_TABLE = \'42P01\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport class RcTablesMissingError extends Error {\\r\\n  constructor() {\\r\\n    super(\\r\\n      \'The RC progress tables are missing. Run supabase/migrations/20260806000000_rc_drilling.sql.\',\\r\\n    );\\r\\n    this.name = \'RcTablesMissingError\';\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction raise(error: { code?: string; message?: string } | null): void {\\r\\n  if (!error) return;\\r\\n  if (error.code === MISSING_TABLE) throw new RcTablesMissingError();\\r\\n  throw new Error(error.message ?? \'Unknown Supabase error\');\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Confirms the progress tables exist. Call once on player/hub boot. */\\r\\nexport async function checkRcTables(): Promise<true> {\\r\\n  const { error } = await rcDb.from(RC_SESSIONS_TABLE).select(\'id\').limit(1);\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n  return true;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function requireUserId(): Promise<string> {\\r\\n  const { data } = await rcDb.auth.getUser();\\r\\n  const id = data?.user?.id;\\r\\n  if (!id) throw new Error(\'You need to be signed in to drill.\');\\r\\n  return id;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction toSession(row: RcDrillSessionRow): RcDrillSession {\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    id: row.id,\\r\\n    userId: row.user_id,\\r\\n    config: row.config as RcDrillConfig,\\r\\n    passageIds: row.passage_ids ?? [],\\r\\n    status: row.status as RcSessionStatus,\\r\\n    excludedFromAnalytics: row.excluded_from_analytics,\\r\\n    startedAt: row.started_at,\\r\\n    completedAt: row.completed_at,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction toAttempt(row: RcAttemptRow): RcAttempt {\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    id: row.id,\\r\\n    sessionId: row.session_id,\\r\\n    userId: row.user_id,\\r\\n    questionId: row.question_id,\\r\\n    passageId: row.passage_id,\\r\\n    selected: (row.selected as RcLetter | null) ?? null,\\r\\n    isCorrect: row.is_correct,\\r\\n    timeMs: row.time_ms,\\r\\n    flagged: row.flagged,\\r\\n    eliminations: (row.eliminations ?? []) as RcLetter[],\\r\\n    isFirstAttempt: row.is_first_attempt,\\r\\n    createdAt: row.created_at,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/* ----------------------------------------------------------------- sessions */\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function createSession(\\r\\n  config: RcDrillConfig,\\r\\n  passageIds: number[],\\r\\n): Promise<RcDrillSession> {\\r\\n  const userId = await requireUserId();\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await rcDb\\r\\n    .from(RC_SESSIONS_TABLE)\\r\\n    .insert({ user_id: userId, config, passage_ids: passageIds })\\r\\n    .select()\\r\\n    .single();\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n  return toSession(data as RcDrillSessionRow);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function getSession(id: string): Promise<RcDrillSession | null> {\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await rcDb.from(RC_SESSIONS_TABLE).select().eq(\'id\', id).maybeSingle();\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n  return data ? toSession(data as RcDrillSessionRow) : null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function listSessions(): Promise<RcDrillSession[]> {\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await rcDb\\r\\n    .from(RC_SESSIONS_TABLE)\\r\\n    .select()\\r\\n    .order(\'started_at\', { ascending: false });\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n  return ((data ?? []) as RcDrillSessionRow[]).map(toSession);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function completeSession(id: string): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  const { error } = await rcDb\\r\\n    .from(RC_SESSIONS_TABLE)\\r\\n    .update({ status: \'completed\', completed_at: new Date().toISOString() })\\r\\n    .eq(\'id\', id);\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function setSessionExcluded(id: string, excluded: boolean): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  const { error } = await rcDb\\r\\n    .from(RC_SESSIONS_TABLE)\\r\\n    .update({ excluded_from_analytics: excluded })\\r\\n    .eq(\'id\', id);\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function deleteSession(id: string): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  const { error } = await rcDb.from(RC_SESSIONS_TABLE).delete().eq(\'id\', id);\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/* ----------------------------------------------------------------- attempts */\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Writes one attempt row. Existing rows are\\r\\n * never updated, so the original answer stays intact.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function recordAttempt(attempt: RcAttempt): Promise<RcAttempt> {\\r\\n  const userId = attempt.userId ?? (await requireUserId());\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { data: prior, error: priorError } = await rcDb\\r\\n    .from(RC_ATTEMPTS_TABLE)\\r\\n    .select(\'id\')\\r\\n    .eq(\'session_id\', attempt.sessionId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'question_id\', attempt.questionId)\\r\\n    .limit(1);\\r\\n  raise(priorError);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const isFirstAttempt = (prior ?? []).length === 0;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await rcDb\\r\\n    .from(RC_ATTEMPTS_TABLE)\\r\\n    .insert({\\r\\n      session_id: attempt.sessionId,\\r\\n      user_id: userId,\\r\\n      question_id: attempt.questionId,\\r\\n      passage_id: attempt.passageId,\\r\\n      selected: attempt.selected,\\r\\n      is_correct: attempt.isCorrect,\\r\\n      time_ms: attempt.timeMs,\\r\\n      flagged: attempt.flagged,\\r\\n      eliminations: attempt.eliminations,\\r\\n      is_first_attempt: isFirstAttempt,\\r\\n    })\\r\\n    .select()\\r\\n    .single();\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n  return toAttempt(data as RcAttemptRow);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function getSessionAttempts(sessionId: string): Promise<RcAttempt[]> {\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await rcDb\\r\\n    .from(RC_ATTEMPTS_TABLE)\\r\\n    .select()\\r\\n    .eq(\'session_id\', sessionId)\\r\\n    .order(\'created_at\', { ascending: true });\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n  return ((data ?? []) as RcAttemptRow[]).map(toAttempt);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Sets the flag on every stored attempt for a question in a session. */\\r\\nexport async function setAttemptFlag(\\r\\n  sessionId: string,\\r\\n  questionId: number,\\r\\n  flagged: boolean,\\r\\n): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  const { error } = await rcDb\\r\\n    .from(RC_ATTEMPTS_TABLE)\\r\\n    .update({ flagged })\\r\\n    .eq(\'session_id\', sessionId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'question_id\', questionId);\\r\\n  raise(error);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Per-question aggregate over the student\'s whole history, used by the hub\'s\\r\\n * status filters and by adaptive selection.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function getQuestionHistory(userId?: string): Promise<RcQuestionHistory> {\\r\\n  const uid = userId ?? ## assistant

The RC launcher has a concrete blocker: its player supports per-passage timers, not a single section timer. The inbox proposal also uses a recipient column that messages do not have, and leaves legacy events without a defined acknowledgement path. I’m checking the visual data requirements and PR dependencies before finishing the review.

## assistant

Correction: I traced the RC timer further. It combines per-passage budgets into one session clock, so my earlier blocker claim was wrong. There is also an existing RC section launcher to reuse; the plan should specify how custom timing maps into it.
